I'll insert the initialized check after `const resolver = getRegistration(name)` and after `if (!resolver)` block, i.e., right before `const lifetime
= ...`. Insert:

```ts
      // If this registration has an initializer, it must have been initialized before
      // it can be resolved. If it has been initialized, return the initialized value.
      if (typeof (resolver as any).initializer === 'function') {
        const initializedValue = findInitializedValue(name)
        if (initializedValue !== undefined) {
          return initializedValue
        }
        throw new AwilixNotInitializedError(name)
      }
```

But careful: `findInitializedValue` returns the value which could legitimately be `undefined`? A service value could be undefined if someone registers
`asValue(undefined)` with initializer? Unlikely. But to be safe, I'll make `findInitializedValue` return `{ found: boolean, value: any }` or use
`hasInitializedValue` first. Let me change design: use `hasInitializedValue(name)` check and then retrieve. But retrieval again needs family walk.
I'll make helper return a sentinel. Actually simplest: define `findInitializedValue` to return `{ value: any } | undefined` and check `!== undefined`.
Since initialized services shouldn't be undefined-valued, fine. But hidden tests might register asClass always object. Fine.

Actually, let me make it cleaner: `findInitializedValue` returns `CacheEntry | undefined`, then `initializedEntry?.value`. But value could be
undefined. Return entry object itself; check `entry !== undefined`. That's robust. Let me define:
```ts
function findInitializedValue(name): CacheEntry | undefined
```
and return `entry.value`.

In resolve:
```ts
const initializedEntry = findInitializedValue(name)
if (initializedEntry !== undefined) {
  return initializedEntry.value
}
```

But `Map.get` returns `undefined` if not found, and also if value stored is `undefined`. We store `CacheEntry` object, so fine.

Now I'll insert all new functions before `dispose()` and update dispose. Let me write the full block.

Important details to get right:

1. `findInitializedValue(name): CacheEntry | undefined` - checks own initializedValues, then parent via symbol.
2. `hasInitializedValue(name): boolean` - similar.
3. `getResolverDependencies(resolver): Array<string|symbol>` - reads `(resolver as any).resolve?.dependencies` which is `Array<Parameter>`, map to
`.name`.
4. `buildInitializationPlan(): Array<{entries: Array<{name, resolver}>}>`
5. `initialize(options)` - state machine.
6. `runInitialization(plan, options)` - async execution.
7. `runLevel` - concurrency pool waiting for all.
8. `setInitializedCacheValue`, `clearInitializedCacheValue`.
9. `rollback`.

Let me think about the type for `Resolver` initializer. The `Resolver` interface doesn't have `initializer` typed (only BuildResolverOptions has it,
and BuildResolver extends it). `rollUpRegistrations()` returns `RegistrationHash` = `Record<..., Resolver<any>>`. When I read `resolver.initializer`,
TypeScript will error because `Resolver` doesn't declare it. I'll cast `(resolver as any).initializer`.

For the plan entry type, I'll define a local interface:
```ts
interface InitializationEntry {
  name: string | symbol
  resolver: Resolver<any>
}
```
Can I declare interface inside function? Yes.

Now the `initialize` state machine and `runInitialization` interplay with `initializationState` assignment.

Let me write:

```ts
  function initialize(options: InitializeOptions = {}): Promise<InitializeResult> {
    if (initializationState.status === 'success') {
      return Promise.resolve(initializationState.result)
    }
    if (initializationState.status === 'failed') {
      return Promise.reject(
        new Error(
          'Cannot re-initialize the container because it previously failed.',
        ),
      )
    }
    if (initializationState.status === 'initializing') {
      return initializationState.promise
    }

    let plan: InitializationLevel[]
    try {
      plan = buildInitializationPlan()
    } catch (err) {
      // Graph construction failures (e.g. cyclic dependencies) must not mark the
      // container as failed - initialize() can be retried.
      return Promise.reject(err)
    }

    const promise = runInitialization(plan, options).then(
      (result) => {
        initializationState = { status: 'success', result }
        return result
      },
      (err) => {
        initializationState = { status: 'failed', error: err as Error }
        throw err
      },
    )
    initializationState = { status: 'initializing', promise }
    return promise
  }
```

Wait: There's a race: `runInitialization` is async and might complete synchronously? No, it's async so `.then` callbacks run in microtask. But
`initializationState = { status: 'initializing', promise }` is set synchronously after creating promise. Good.

But there's a subtle bug: if `runInitialization` rejects, we set state failed in the rejection handler. Good.

Now `buildInitializationPlan`:

```ts
  interface InitializationLevel {
    entries: Array<InitializationEntry>
  }

  function buildInitializationPlan(): InitializationLevel[] {
    const entries: InitializationEntry[] = []
    const localNames = [
      ...Object.keys(registrations),
      ...Object.getOwnPropertySymbols(registrations),
    ]
    for (const name of localNames) {
      const resolver = registrations[name as any] as Resolver<any>
      if (resolver && typeof (resolver as any).initializer === 'function') {
        entries.push({ name, resolver })
      }
    }

    if (entries.length === 0) {
      return []
    }

    const byName = new Map<string | symbol, InitializationEntry>()
    for (const entry of entries) {
      byName.set(entry.name, entry)
    }

    // Map each initialized registration to the initialized registrations it depends on.
    const dependencies = new Map<string | symbol, Array<string | symbol>>()
    for (const entry of entries) {
      const deps = getResolverDependencies(entry.resolver).filter((depName) =>
        byName.has(depName),
      )
      dependencies.set(entry.name, deps)
    }

    // Detect cycles using DFS.
    const visiting = new Set<string | symbol>()
    const visited = new Set<string | symbol>()
    const path: ResolutionStack = []

    const visit = (name: string | symbol) => {
      if (visiting.has(name)) {
        throw new AwilixResolutionError(
          name,
          path,
          'Cyclic dependencies detected.',
        )
      }
      if (visited.has(name)) {
        return
      }
      visiting.add(name)
      path.push({
        name,
        lifetime: (byName.get(name)!.resolver.lifetime || Lifetime.TRANSIENT) as LifetimeType,
      })
      for (const depName of dependencies.get(name) || []) {
        visit(depName)
      }
      path.pop()
      visiting.delete(name)
      visited.add(name)
    }

    for (const entry of entries) {
      visit(entry.name)
    }

    // Compute levels.
    const levels: InitializationLevel[] = []
    const processed = new Set<string | symbol>()
    const remaining = new Set<string | symbol>(entries.map((e) => e.name))

    while (remaining.size > 0) {
      const levelEntries: Array<InitializationEntry> = []
      for (const name of remaining) {
        const deps = dependencies.get(name) || []
        if (deps.every((depName) => processed.has(depName))) {
          levelEntries.push(byName.get(name)!)
        }
      }
      if (levelEntries.length === 0) {
        // Defensive: should never happen after cycle detection.
        const first = remaining.values().next().value as string | symbol
        throw new AwilixResolutionError(
          first,
          [],
          'Cyclic dependencies detected.',
        )
      }
      for (const entry of levelEntries) {
        processed.add(entry.name)
        remaining.delete(entry.name)
      }
      levels.push({ entries: levelEntries })
    }

    return levels
  }
```

Note: `getResolverDependencies`:

```ts
  function getResolverDependencies(resolver: Resolver<any>): Array<string | symbol> {
    const resolve = (resolver as any).resolve
    const dependencies = resolve && resolve.dependencies
    if (!Array.isArray(dependencies)) {
      return []
    }
    return dependencies.map((dep: { name: string | symbol }) => dep.name)
  }
```

`runInitialization`:

```ts
  async function runInitialization(
    levels: InitializationLevel[],
    options: InitializeOptions,
  ): Promise<InitializeResult> {
    const start = performance.now()
    const metrics: Record<string | symbol, InitializeMetrics> = {}
    const initializedStack: Array<InitializationEntry & { value: any }> = []

    try {
      for (let levelIndex = 0; levelIndex < levels.length; levelIndex++) {
        const level = levels[levelIndex]
        await runLevel(level.entries, options.concurrency ?? Infinity, async (entry) => {
          const metricStart = performance.now()
          let value: any
          try {
            const instance = entry.resolver.resolve(container)
            const initializer = (entry.resolver as any).initializer as Initializer<any>
            const result = await initializer(instance)
            value = result === undefined ? instance : result
          } catch (err) {
            throw new AwilixInitializationError(entry.name, err as Error)
          }

          initializedValues.set(entry.name, { resolver: entry.resolver, value })
          setInitializedCacheValue(entry.name, entry.resolver, value)
          initializedStack.push({ ...entry, value })
          metrics[entry.name] = {
            duration: performance.now() - metricStart,
            level: levelIndex,
          }
        })
      }

      return {
        totalDuration: performance.now() - start,
        metrics,
      }
    } catch (err) {
      await rollbackInitialized(initializedStack)
      throw err
    }
  }
```

Wait, there's a subtle issue: if `entry.resolver.resolve(container)` succeeds but `initializer` is not a function (shouldn't happen since plan
filters), then `await initializer(instance)` would error. Fine.

Another issue: `resolver.resolve(container)` for a singleton caches the ORIGINAL instance. If another task in the same level resolves the same
singleton (e.g., two independent services both depend on it) — but if it has an initializer and is in this level, that would mean a dependency on a
same-level service, which shouldn't happen in a correct graph. If it's a non-initializer service that gets resolved as a dependency and happens to be
in cache with original uninitialized value... only if the service has an initializer and is in this level. Not an issue for correct graphs.

But there's another issue: `entry.resolver.resolve(container)` for a service whose **dependency** has an initializer at an earlier level. That
dependency's initialized value is in `initializedValues`, so when the dependency is resolved via `container.resolve(depName)`, our modified resolve
returns the initialized value. Good.

However, `entry.resolver.resolve(container)` for a service that depends on **another service in the same level** would call
`container.resolve(depName)` and hit NotInitialized (since dep not yet in initializedValues). That would throw AwilixInitializationError for the
current entry. In a correct graph, same-level services don't depend on each other. Good.

Now `setInitializedCacheValue`:

```ts
  function setInitializedCacheValue(
    name: string | symbol,
    resolver: Resolver<any>,
    value: any,
  ): void {
    const lifetime = resolver.lifetime || Lifetime.TRANSIENT
    const targetCache =
      lifetime === Lifetime.SINGLETON ? rootContainer.cache : container.cache
    targetCache.set(name, { resolver, value })
  }
```

Wait, but for singleton, if the singleton is owned by this container and was resolved during init, rootContainer.cache already has an entry (with
original value) set by `resolver.resolve(container)`. We overwrite with initialized value. Good.

For transient, we set container.cache. This means if user calls `container.dispose()`, it will dispose the initialized transient. Good.

`clearInitializedCacheValue`:
```ts
  function clearInitializedCacheValue(name: string | symbol): void {
    container.cache.delete(name)
    if (rootContainer.cache !== container.cache) {
      rootContainer.cache.delete(name)
    }
  }
```

`rollbackInitialized`:
```ts
  async function rollbackInitialized(
    stack: Array<InitializationEntry & { value: any }>,
  ): Promise<void> {
    for (let i = stack.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
      const { name, resolver, value } = stack[i]
      const disposable = resolver as DisposableResolver<any>
      if (disposable.dispose) {
        try {
          await disposable.dispose(value)
        } catch {
          // Disposer errors must not override the original initialization error.
        }
      }
      initializedValues.delete(name)
      clearInitializedCacheValue(name)
    }
  }
```

Wait, should we clear initializedValues before or after disposal? Doesn't matter much; do after disposal per entry so resolve during rollback still
blocked. Actually if we clear after each, fine.

But there's a subtlety: If a disposer itself resolves the service (weird), it would get NotInitialized if already cleared. Do clear after disposal.
Good.

`runLevel`:

```ts
  async function runLevel<T>(
    entries: Array<T>,
    concurrency: number,
    task: (entry: T) => Promise<void>,
  ): Promise<void> {
    if (entries.length === 0) {
      return
    }
    let index = 0
    const errors: unknown[] = []
    const workerCount = Math.max(1, Math.min(concurrency, entries.length))
    const workers = Array.from({ length: workerCount }, async () => {
      while (index < entries.length) {
        const current = index++
        try {
          await task(entries[current])
        } catch (err) {
          errors.push(err)
        }
      }
    })
    await Promise.all(workers)
    if (errors.length > 0) {
      throw errors[0]
    }
  }
```

Note: `concurrency` could be 0? If user passes 0, workerCount = max(1, min(0, n)) = 1. That's fine, treat as 1.

Now `findInitializedValue` and `hasInitializedValue`:

```ts
  function findInitializedValue(
    name: string | symbol,
  ): CacheEntry | undefined {
    const entry = initializedValues.get(name)
    if (entry !== undefined) {
      return entry
    }
    if (parentContainer) {
      return (parentContainer as any)[FIND_INITIALIZED_VALUE](name)
    }
    return undefined
  }

  function hasInitializedValue(name: string | symbol): boolean {
    if (initializedValues.has(name)) {
      return true
    }
    if (parentContainer) {
      return (parentContainer as any)[HAS_INITIALIZED_VALUE](name)
    }
    return false
  }
```

Wait, do I actually use `hasInitializedValue` anywhere? In resolve I use `findInitializedValue` and check `!== undefined`. So `hasInitializedValue`
might be unused → `noUnusedLocals` error. But it's attached to container object so it's used there. Actually attaching to container object counts as
usage. But let me check: `[HAS_INITIALIZED_VALUE]: hasInitializedValue` references it. Yes used.

Actually, I realize there might be an issue with `findInitializedValue` returning `undefined` when the stored value itself is `undefined`. We store
`CacheEntry` objects, not raw values, so `initializedValues.get(name)` returns the entry object or undefined. Fine.

Now, one more important consideration: **The resolve early-return happens before strict-mode lifetime check and before pushing resolution stack.**
That means if an initialized singleton is resolved from a child and the child's strict mode would flag a lifetime leak, it won't. Acceptable.

But wait, there's a subtle issue with `findInitializedValue` and **same-name local vs parent**. `initializedValues` keys are names. If parent
initialized `db` (singleton), child's `findInitializedValue('db')` finds it via parent. Good. If child registers its own `db` with initializer and
child initialized it, child's map has it first. Good.

Now, another big issue: **During `runInitialization`, when we call `entry.resolver.resolve(container)`, for a singleton, it caches into
`rootContainer.cache` BEFORE initializer completes. If a dependency of another entry in the same or later level resolves it before init completes, it
gets uninitialized value.** For correct levels, a service with initializer at level N is only depended on by services at level > N. So by the time
later levels run, it's initialized and `initializedValues` early-return gives initialized value. But what about a **non-initializer service at level
N+1** that depends on the initialized service? When that non-initializer service is resolved (as a dependency of an initializer at N+1), it calls
`container.resolve('initService')` → early return initialized value. Good.

But what about **direct resolution by user** during initialization (e.g., in an initializer, someone calls `container.resolve('someOther')`)? Edge
case. Fine.

Now let me also handle the **`resolver.resolve` for a service whose initializer returns a replacement that has different lifetime/disposer**. We
update cache with replacement value, so dispose uses replacement. Good.

Now, let me also think about **the case where the same registration appears in `initializedValues` but was initialized by a previous successful
`initialize()` call, and then user calls `initialize()` again** — state success returns immediately, doesn't re-run. Good.

**What about after `dispose()`?** I'll reset initializationState to idle and clear initializedValues. Let me update dispose.

Let me also think: `dispose()` currently uses `Promise.all` which disposes in parallel. That's existing behavior; I won't change ordering for normal
dispose. But for rollback we use reverse sequential.

when 'service' resolve continues and eventually returns/pops, stack is fine. Good.

block = r'''
  /**
   * Finds an initialized value for a registration, walking up the parent
   * chain if needed.
   */
  function findInitializedValue(
    name: string | symbol,
  ): CacheEntry | undefined {
    const entry = initializedValues.get(name)
    if (entry !== undefined) {
      return entry
    }
    if (parentContainer) {
      return (parentContainer as any)[FIND_INITIALIZED_VALUE](name)
    }
    return undefined
  }

  /**
   * Checks if a registration has been initialized in this container or any parent.
   */
  function hasInitializedValue(name: string | symbol): boolean {
    if (initializedValues.has(name)) {
      return true
    }
    if (parentContainer) {
      return (parentContainer as any)[HAS_INITIALIZED_VALUE](name)
    }
    return false
  }

  /**
   * A single registration in an initialization plan.
   */
  interface InitializationEntry {
    name: string | symbol
    resolver: Resolver<any>
  }

  /**
   * A level in an initialization plan. All entries in a level are initialized
   * concurrently before the next level begins.
   */
  interface InitializationLevel {
    entries: Array<InitializationEntry>
  }

  /**
   * Extracts dependency names from a resolver. Dependencies are parsed when the
   * resolver is created and exposed on the resolve function.
   */
  function getResolverDependencies(
    resolver: Resolver<any>,
  ): Array<string | symbol> {
    const resolve = (resolver as any).resolve
    const dependencies = resolve && resolve.dependencies
    if (!Array.isArray(dependencies)) {
      return []
    }
    return dependencies.map(
      (dep: { name: string | symbol }) => dep.name,
    )
  }

  /**
   * Builds the initialization plan: registrations with an initializer grouped
   * into dependency levels. Throws AwilixResolutionError if a cycle is detected.
   */
  function buildInitializationPlan(): InitializationLevel[] {
    const entries: Array<InitializationEntry> = []
    const localNames = [
      ...Object.keys(registrations),
      ...Object.getOwnPropertySymbols(registrations),
    ]
    for (const name of localNames) {
      const resolver = registrations[name as any] as Resolver<any>
      if (resolver && typeof (resolver as any).initializer === 'function') {
        entries.push({ name, resolver })
      }
    }

    if (entries.length === 0) {
      return []
    }

    const byName = new Map<string | symbol, InitializationEntry>()
    for (const entry of entries) {
      byName.set(entry.name, entry)
    }

    // Map each initialized registration to the initialized registrations it
    // depends on.
    const dependencies = new Map<
      string | symbol,
      Array<string | symbol>
    >()
    for (const entry of entries) {
      const deps = getResolverDependencies(entry.resolver).filter(
        (depName) => byName.has(depName),
      )
      dependencies.set(entry.name, deps)
    }

    // Detect cycles using DFS.
    const visiting = new Set<string | symbol>()
    const visited = new Set<string | symbol>()
    const path: ResolutionStack = []

    const visit = (name: string | symbol) => {
      if (visiting.has(name)) {
        throw new AwilixResolutionError(
          name,
          path,
          'Cyclic dependencies detected.',
        )
      }
      if (visited.has(name)) {
        return
      }
      visiting.add(name)
      path.push({
        name,
        lifetime:
          (byName.get(name)!.resolver.lifetime ||
            Lifetime.TRANSIENT) as LifetimeType,
      })
      for (const depName of dependencies.get(name) || []) {
        visit(depName)
      }
      path.pop()
      visiting.delete(name)
      visited.add(name)
    }

    for (const entry of entries) {
      visit(entry.name)
    }

    // Compute levels: a registration is in the current level once all of its
    // initialized dependencies have been processed.
    const levels: InitializationLevel[] = []
    const processed = new Set<string | symbol>()
    const remaining = new Set<string | symbol>(entries.map((e) => e.name))

    while (remaining.size > 0) {
      const levelEntries: Array<InitializationEntry> = []
      for (const name of remaining) {
        const deps = dependencies.get(name) || []
        if (deps.every((depName) => processed.has(depName))) {
          levelEntries.push(byName.get(name)!)
        }
      }
      if (levelEntries.length === 0) {
        // Defensive; cycles are already detected above.
        const first = remaining.values().next().value as string | symbol
        throw new AwilixResolutionError(
          first,
          [],
          'Cyclic dependencies detected.',
        )
      }
      for (const entry of levelEntries) {
        processed.add(entry.name)
        remaining.delete(entry.name)
      }
      levels.push({ entries: levelEntries })
    }

    return levels
  }

  /**
   * Initializes the container. Resolves all registrations with an `initializer`
   * in dependency order, running initializers within a level concurrently.
   */
  function initialize(options: InitializeOptions = {}): Promise<InitializeResult> {
    if (initializationState.status === 'success') {
      return Promise.resolve(initializationState.result)
    }
    if (initializationState.status === 'failed') {
      return Promise.reject(
        new Error(
          'Cannot re-initialize the container because it previously failed.',
        ),
      )
    }
    if (initializationState.status === 'initializing') {
      return initializationState.promise
    }

    let plan: Array<InitializationLevel>
    try {
      plan = buildInitializationPlan()
    } catch (err) {
      // Graph construction failures (e.g. cyclic dependencies) must not mark
      // the container as failed, allowing initialize() to be retried.
      return Promise.reject(err)
    }

    const promise = runInitialization(plan, options).then(
      (result) => {
        initializationState = { status: 'success', result }
        return result
      },
      (err) => {
        initializationState = { status: 'failed', error: err as Error }
        throw err
      },
    )
    initializationState = { status: 'initializing', promise }
    return promise
  }

  /**
   * Runs a level of initializers, waiting for all in-flight initializers to
   * complete before throwing. If any initializer fails, the first error is
   * rethrown after all tasks have settled.
   */
  async function runLevel<T>(
    entries: Array<T>,
    concurrency: number,
    task: (entry: T) => Promise<void>,
  ): Promise<void> {
    if (entries.length === 0) {
      return
    }
    let index = 0
    const errors: unknown[] = []
    const workerCount = Math.max(1, Math.min(concurrency, entries.length))
    const workers = Array.from({ length: workerCount }, async () => {
      while (index < entries.length) {
        const current = index++
        try {
          await task(entries[current])
        } catch (err) {
          errors.push(err)
        }
      }
    })
    await Promise.all(workers)
    if (errors.length > 0) {
      throw errors[0]
    }
  }

  /**
   * Stores an initialized value in the appropriate lifetime cache.
   */
  function setInitializedCacheValue(
    name: string | symbol,
    resolver: Resolver<any>,
    value: any,
  ): void {
    const lifetime = resolver.lifetime || Lifetime.TRANSIENT
    const targetCache =
      lifetime === Lifetime.SINGLETON ? rootContainer.cache : container.cache
    targetCache.set(name, { resolver, value })
  }

  /**
   * Removes an initialized value from the lifetime caches.
   */
  function clearInitializedCacheValue(name: string | symbol): void {
    container.cache.delete(name)
    if (rootContainer.cache !== container.cache) {
      rootContainer.cache.delete(name)
    }
  }

  /**
   * Rolls back already-initialized services in reverse order. Disposer errors
   * are ignored so they never override the original initialization error.
   */
  async function rollbackInitialized(
    stack: Array<InitializationEntry & { value: any }>,
  ): Promise<void> {
    for (let i = stack.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
      const { name, resolver, value } = stack[i]
      const disposable = resolver as DisposableResolver<any>
      if (disposable.dispose) {
        try {
          await disposable.dispose(value)
        } catch {
          // Ignore disposer errors during rollback.
        }
      }
      initializedValues.delete(name)
      clearInitializedCacheValue(name)
    }
  }

  /**
   * Executes the initialization plan and returns metrics.
   */
  async function runInitialization(
    levels: Array<InitializationLevel>,
    options: InitializeOptions,
  ): Promise<InitializeResult> {
    const start = performance.now()
    const metrics: Record<string | symbol, InitializeMetrics> = {}
    const initializedStack: Array<
      InitializationEntry & { value: any }
    > = []

    try {
      for (
        let levelIndex = 0;
        levelIndex < levels.length;
        levelIndex++
      ) {
        const level = levels[levelIndex]
        await runLevel(
          level.entries,
          options.concurrency ?? Infinity,
          async (entry) => {
            const metricStart = performance.now()
            let value: any
            try {
              const instance = entry.resolver.resolve(container)
              const initializer = (entry.resolver as any)
                .initializer as Initializer<any>
              const result = await initializer(instance)
              value = result === undefined ? instance : result
            } catch (err) {
              throw new AwilixInitializationError(
                entry.name,
                err as Error,
              )
            }

            initializedValues.set(entry.name, {
              resolver: entry.resolver,
              value,
            })
            setInitializedCacheValue(entry.name, entry.resolver, value)
            initializedStack.push({ ...entry, value })
            metrics[entry.name] = {
              duration: performance.now() - metricStart,
              level: levelIndex,
            }
          },
        )
      }

      return {
        totalDuration: performance.now() - start,
        metrics,
      }
    } catch (err) {
      await rollbackInitialized(initializedStack)
      throw err
    }
  }

  /**
   * Disposes this container and it's children, calling the disposer
   * on all disposable registrations and clearing the cache.
   */
  function dispose(): Promise<void> {
    const entries = Array.from(container.cache.entries())
    container.cache.clear()
    initializedValues.clear()
    initializationState = { status: 'idle' }
    return Promise.all(
      entries.map(([, entry]) => {
        const { resolver, value } = entry
        const disposable = resolver as DisposableResolver<any>
        if (disposable.dispose) {
          return Promise.resolve().then(() => disposable.dispose!(value))
        }
        return Promise.resolve()
      }),
    ).then(() => undefined)
  }
}
'''

""",
"""        throw new AwilixResolutionError(name, resolutionStack)

      // If this registration has an initializer, it must be initialized before it can be
      // resolved. If it has already been initialized, return the initialized value.

        const initializedEntry = findInitializedValue(name)
        if (initializedEntry !== undefined) {
          return initializedEntry.value
        }
        throw new AwilixNotInitializedError(name)
      }

      const lifetime = resolver.lifetime || Lifetime.TRANSIENT

p='src/container.ts'
s=open(p).read()

marker = """  /**
   * Disposes this container and it's children, calling the disposer
   * on all disposable registrations and clearing the cache.
   */
  function dispose(): Promise<void> {"""

block = r'''
  /**
   * Finds an initialized value for a registration, walking up the parent
   * chain if needed.
   */
  function findInitializedValue(
    name: string | symbol,
  ): CacheEntry | undefined {
    const entry = initializedValues.get(name)
    if (entry !== undefined) {
      return entry
    }
    if (parentContainer) {
      return (parentContainer as any)[FIND_INITIALIZED_VALUE](name)
    }
    return undefined
  }

  /**
   * Checks if a registration has been initialized in this container or any parent.
   */
  function hasInitializedValue(name: string | symbol): boolean {
    if (initializedValues.has(name)) {
      return true
    }
    if (parentContainer) {
      return (parentContainer as any)[HAS_INITIALIZED_VALUE](name)
    }
    return false
  }

  /**
   * A single registration in an initialization plan.
   */
  interface InitializationEntry {
    name: string | symbol
    resolver: Resolver<any>
  }

  /**
   * A level in an initialization plan. All entries in a level are initialized
   * concurrently before the next level begins.
   */
  interface InitializationLevel {
    entries: Array<InitializationEntry>
  }

  /**
   * Extracts dependency names from a resolver. Dependencies are parsed when the
   * resolver is created and exposed on the resolve function.
   */
  function getResolverDependencies(
    resolver: Resolver<any>,
  ): Array<string | symbol> {
    const resolve = (resolver as any).resolve
    const dependencies = resolve && resolve.dependencies
    if (!Array.isArray(dependencies)) {
      return []
    }
    return dependencies.map(
      (dep: { name: string | symbol }) => dep.name,
    )
  }

  /**
   * Builds the initialization plan: registrations with an initializer grouped
   * into dependency levels. Throws AwilixResolutionError if a cycle is detected.
   */
  function buildInitializationPlan(): Array<InitializationLevel> {
    const entries: Array<InitializationEntry> = []
    const localNames = [
      ...Object.keys(registrations),
      ...Object.getOwnPropertySymbols(registrations),
    ]
    for (const name of localNames) {
      const resolver = registrations[name as any] as Resolver<any>
      if (resolver && typeof (resolver as any).initializer === 'function') {
        entries.push({ name, resolver })
      }
    }

    if (entries.length === 0) {
      return []
    }

    const byName = new Map<string | symbol, InitializationEntry>()
    for (const entry of entries) {
      byName.set(entry.name, entry)
    }

    // Map each initialized registration to the initialized registrations it
    // depends on.
    const dependencies = new Map<
      string | symbol,
      Array<string | symbol>
    >()
    for (const entry of entries) {
      const deps = getResolverDependencies(entry.resolver).filter(
        (depName) => byName.has(depName),
      )
      dependencies.set(entry.name, deps)
    }

    // Detect cycles using DFS.
    const visiting = new Set<string | symbol>()
    const visited = new Set<string | symbol>()
    const path: ResolutionStack = []

    const visit = (name: string | symbol) => {
      if (visiting.has(name)) {
        throw new AwilixResolutionError(
          name,
          path,
          'Cyclic dependencies detected.',
        )
      }
      if (visited.has(name)) {
        return
      }
      visiting.add(name)
      path.push({
        name,
        lifetime:
          (byName.get(name)!.resolver.lifetime ||
            Lifetime.TRANSIENT) as LifetimeType,
      })
      for (const depName of dependencies.get(name) || []) {
        visit(depName)
      }
      path.pop()
      visiting.delete(name)
      visited.add(name)
    }

    for (const entry of entries) {
      visit(entry.name)
    }

    // Compute levels: a registration is in the current level once all of its
    // initialized dependencies have been processed.
    const levels: Array<InitializationLevel> = []
    const processed = new Set<string | symbol>()
    const remaining = new Set<string | symbol>(entries.map((e) => e.name))

    while (remaining.size > 0) {
      const levelEntries: Array<InitializationEntry> = []
      for (const name of remaining) {
        const deps = dependencies.get(name) || []
        if (deps.every((depName) => processed.has(depName))) {
          levelEntries.push(byName.get(name)!)
        }
      }
      if (levelEntries.length === 0) {
        // Defensive; cycles are already detected above.
        const first = remaining.values().next().value as string | symbol
        throw new AwilixResolutionError(
          first,
          [],
          'Cyclic dependencies detected.',
        )
      }
      for (const entry of levelEntries) {
        processed.add(entry.name)
        remaining.delete(entry.name)
      }
      levels.push({ entries: levelEntries })
    }

    return levels
  }

  /**
   * Initializes the container. Resolves all registrations with an `initializer`
   * in dependency order, running initializers within a level concurrently.
   */
  function initialize(
    options: InitializeOptions = {},
  ): Promise<InitializeResult> {
    if (initializationState.status === 'success') {
      return Promise.resolve(initializationState.result)
    }
    if (initializationState.status === 'failed') {
      return Promise.reject(
        new Error(
          'Cannot re-initialize the container because it previously failed.',
        ),
      )
    }
    if (initializationState.status === 'initializing') {
      return initializationState.promise
    }

    let plan: Array<InitializationLevel>
    try {
      plan = buildInitializationPlan()
    } catch (err) {
      // Graph construction failures (e.g. cyclic dependencies) must not mark
      // the container as failed, allowing initialize() to be retried.
      return Promise.reject(err)
    }

    const promise = runInitialization(plan, options).then(
      (result) => {
        initializationState = { status: 'success', result }
        return result
      },
      (err) => {
        initializationState = { status: 'failed', error: err as Error }
        throw err
      },
    )
    initializationState = { status: 'initializing', promise }
    return promise
  }

  /**
   * Runs a level of initializers, waiting for all in-flight initializers to
   * complete before throwing. If any initializer fails, the first error is
   * rethrown after all tasks have settled.
   */
  async function runLevel<T>(
    entries: Array<T>,
    concurrency: number,
    task: (entry: T) => Promise<void>,
  ): Promise<void> {
    if (entries.length === 0) {
      return
    }
    let index = 0
    const errors: unknown[] = []
    const workerCount = Math.max(1, Math.min(concurrency, entries.length))
    const workers = Array.from({ length: workerCount }, async () => {
      while (index < entries.length) {
        const current = index++
        try {
          await task(entries[current])
        } catch (err) {
          errors.push(err)
        }
      }
    })
    await Promise.all(workers)
    if (errors.length > 0) {
      throw errors[0]
    }
  }

  /**
   * Stores an initialized value in the appropriate lifetime cache.
   */
  function setInitializedCacheValue(
    name: string | symbol,
    resolver: Resolver<any>,
    value: any,
  ): void {
    const lifetime = resolver.lifetime || Lifetime.TRANSIENT
    const targetCache =
      lifetime === Lifetime.SINGLETON ? rootContainer.cache : container.cache
    targetCache.set(name, { resolver, value })
  }

  /**
   * Removes an initialized value from the lifetime caches.
   */
  function clearInitializedCacheValue(name: string | symbol): void {
    container.cache.delete(name)
    if (rootContainer.cache !== container.cache) {
      rootContainer.cache.delete(name)
    }
  }

  /**
   * Rolls back already-initialized services in reverse order. Disposer errors
   * are ignored so they never override the original initialization error.
   */
  async function rollbackInitialized(
    stack: Array<InitializationEntry & { value: any }>,
  ): Promise<void> {
    for (let i = stack.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
      const { name, resolver, value } = stack[i]
      const disposable = resolver as DisposableResolver<any>
      if (disposable.dispose) {
        try {
          await disposable.dispose(value)
        } catch {
          // Ignore disposer errors during rollback.
        }
      }
      initializedValues.delete(name)
      clearInitializedCacheValue(name)
    }
  }

  /**
   * Executes the initialization plan and returns metrics.
   */
  async function runInitialization(
    levels: Array<InitializationLevel>,
    options: InitializeOptions,
  ): Promise<InitializeResult> {
    const start = performance.now()
    const metrics: Record<string | symbol, InitializeMetrics> = {}
    const initializedStack: Array<
      InitializationEntry & { value: any }
    > = []

    try {
      for (
        let levelIndex = 0;
        levelIndex < levels.length;
        levelIndex++
      ) {
        const level = levels[levelIndex]
        await runLevel(
          level.entries,
          options.concurrency ?? Infinity,
          async (entry) => {
            const metricStart = performance.now()
            let value: any
            try {
              const instance = entry.resolver.resolve(container)
              const initializer = (entry.resolver as any)
                .initializer as Initializer<any>
              const result = await initializer(instance)
              value = result === undefined ? instance : result
            } catch (err) {
              throw new AwilixInitializationError(
                entry.name,
                err as Error,
              )
            }

            initializedValues.set(entry.name, {
              resolver: entry.resolver,
              value,
            })
            setInitializedCacheValue(entry.name, entry.resolver, value)
            initializedStack.push({ ...entry, value })
            metrics[entry.name] = {
              duration: performance.now() - metricStart,
              level: levelIndex,
            }
          },
        )
      }

      return {
        totalDuration: performance.now() - start,
        metrics,
      }
    } catch (err) {
      await rollbackInitialized(initializedStack)
      throw err
    }
  }

  /**
   * Disposes this container and it's children, calling the disposer
   * on all disposable registrations and clearing the cache.
   */
  function dispose(): Promise<void> {'''

Several issues:

1. `initializedValues` used in container object literal before declaration. The `const initializedValues` is declared after `rootContainer`, which is
after the container object literal. Need to declare it before the container object. Also `findInitializedValue` and `hasInitializedValue` are function
declarations (hoisted) so fine. Move `initializedValues` and `initializationState` declaration before the container object. But `initializationState`
type references `InitializeResult` — fine. Let me move the state declarations to before the container object (e.g., near `resolutionStack` or just
before `const container = {`).

2. `BuildResolver<T> extends BuildResolverOptions<T>` conflict: `BuildResolver` now has method `initializer(initializer: Initializer<T>): this` while
`BuildResolverOptions` has property `initializer?: Initializer<T>`. This is a conflict because interfaces extend. In the original code,
`BuildResolver` extends `BuildResolverOptions` and also declares methods like `inject`, `dispose` etc. The `dispose` conflict is handled by
`DisposableResolverOptions` having `dispose?: Disposer<T>` and `DisposableResolver` interface having `disposer(dispose: ...)` (different name, so no
conflict). But I added method `initializer` same name as property `initializer`.

How does real awilix handle this? In real awilix, `BuildResolver` doesn't extend `BuildResolverOptions`? Let me check original: `export interface
BuildResolver<T> extends Resolver<T>, BuildResolverOptions<T> { ... }`. Yes it does. The real awilix added `initializer(initializer: Initializer<T>):
this` too, and it must have resolved the conflict. Let me recall: In TypeScript, an interface can extend another and declare a property with the same
name as long as types are compatible. Here method type `(initializer: Initializer<T>) => this` vs `Initializer<T>` = `(instance: T) => T |
Promise<T>`. Incompatible.

Maybe real awilix removed `initializer` from `BuildResolverOptions` and put it only on the interface? Let me check: `BuildResolverOptions` is used as
the options type for `asFunction`/`asClass` opts argument. It needs `initializer?: Initializer<T>` so users can pass `{ initializer: fn }`. But the
interface conflict arises only because BuildResolver extends BuildResolverOptions and also declares method with same name.

Options:
- Rename the method to something else? No, API requires `.initializer(...)`.
- Remove `initializer?: Initializer<T>` from `BuildResolverOptions` and add it to a new interface? But then passing `{ initializer }` as opts wouldn't
typecheck. We could keep it in BuildResolverOptions but not have BuildResolver extend it? That would be a bigger refactor.
- Instead, declare `BuildResolver` with an `initializer` that is a union? Not really.

Actually, how does `inject` work? `BuildResolverOptions` has `injector?: InjectorFunction` and `BuildResolver` has method `inject(injector:
InjectorFunction): this`. Different names. And `dispose` — `BuildResolverOptions` extends `DisposableResolverOptions` which has `dispose?:
Disposer<T>`; `BuildResolver` does NOT declare `dispose`, only `disposer`. So no conflict.

I think the cleanest fix: remove `initializer` from `BuildResolverOptions` (or rename to not conflict), and instead define a separate
`InitializableResolverOptions`? But then `asFunction(fn, { initializer })` wouldn't accept it. Hmm.

Alternative: Keep `initializer?: Initializer<T>` in BuildResolverOptions but make `BuildResolver` NOT extend BuildResolverOptions; instead have it
extend a modified version. Actually let me check what the real awilix does — I believe the real `BuildResolver` interface declares
`initializer(initializer: Initializer<T>): this` and `BuildResolverOptions` has `initializer?: Initializer<T>`. Wait, does TS allow overriding a
property with a function type? Method declaration in interface is a property whose type is a function `(initializer: Initializer<T>) => this`. The
base has `Initializer<T>`. These are incompatible because return type `this` vs `T | Promise<T>`? `this` is not assignable to `T` necessarily. So TS
would complain.

Let me actually check the real awilix source to see how they did it. I recall the real `BuildResolver` interface:
```ts
export interface BuildResolver<T> extends Resolver<T>, BuildResolverOptions<T> {
  injectionMode?: InjectionModeType
  injector?: InjectorFunction
  setLifetime(lifetime: LifetimeType): this
  setInjectionMode(mode: InjectionModeType): this
  singleton(): this
  scoped(): this
  transient(): this
  proxy(): this
  classic(): this
  inject(injector: InjectorFunction): this
  disposer(dispose: Disposer<T>): this
}
```
And `BuildResolverOptions`:
```ts
export interface BuildResolverOptions<T>
  extends ResolverOptions<T>,
    DisposableResolverOptions<T> {
  injectionMode?: InjectionModeType
  injector?: InjectorFunction
  initializer?: Initializer<T>
}
```
And I think `BuildResolver` doesn't declare `initializer` as a method — instead, `createBuildResolver` returns a type that has both the method and the
options. Hmm but then `.initializer()` would not typecheck on `BuildResolver`.

Wait, maybe in the real awilix, `BuildResolver` is defined as:
```ts
export interface BuildResolver<T> extends Resolver<T>, BuildResolverOptions<T> {
  ...
  initializer(initializer: Initializer<T>): this
}
```
and `BuildResolverOptions` does NOT include initializer; instead there's a separate `InitializerOptions`? Let me think.

Actually, I can resolve the conflict by making `initializer` in `BuildResolverOptions` a method-compatible type? No.

Simplest approach: Don't add `initializer?` to `BuildResolverOptions`. Instead, add it to a new interface `InitializerResolverOptions<T>` or just add
it directly to `Resolver` interface as an optional property? The `Resolver` interface is used broadly; adding `initializer?: Initializer<T>` there is
fine and avoids conflict because `BuildResolver` extends `Resolver` (property) and declares method (same conflict!). Same issue if `BuildResolver`
extends both.

Hmm. The conflict is specifically because `BuildResolver` both extends a type with property `initializer` and declares method `initializer`. To fix,
either:
(a) `BuildResolver` doesn't extend a type that has `initializer` property, and instead declares `initializer?: Initializer<T>` plus method? Can't have
both.
(b) Don't declare method in `BuildResolver`; instead make `createBuildResolver` return type include method via intersection. But the return type is
`BuildResolver<T> & B`; if `BuildResolver` doesn't have method, `.initializer()` won't be in type. We could add a separate interface
`InitializerResolver<T>` with the method, and make `asFunction`/`asClass` return `BuildResolver<T> & DisposableResolver<T> & InitializerResolver<T>`.
But then users' variables typed as `BuildResolver<T>` won't have `.initializer()`. But in practice, the returned value from `asFunction()` is the
intersection, so `.initializer()` works. And `container.register` accepts `Resolver<T>`, and `BuildResolver` still has `initializer?:` property for
reading.

Actually let me check how the real awilix types it. I'll try to find in the actual package via memory: In awilix README, the API shows:
```ts
container.register({
  db: asClass(Database).singleton().initializer(async (instance) => { ... })
})
```
So `.initializer()` must be on the returned type.

Let me look at what approach causes least disruption:
- Keep `initializer?: Initializer<T>` in `BuildResolverOptions`.
- Remove the `initializer(initializer): this` declaration from `BuildResolver` interface.
- Add `initializer(initializer: Initializer<T>): this` to a new interface `BuildResolverWithInitializer<T>` and make `asFunction`/`asClass` return
`BuildResolver<T> & DisposableResolver<T> & BuildResolverWithInitializer<T>`.

But wait — `createBuildResolver` currently returns `BuildResolver<T> & B`. If I want `initializer()` method on it, I could add it to the
`BuildResolver` interface but as a **method with a compatible type**. The issue is property type conflict. What if the property in
`BuildResolverOptions` is typed to accept both? Not possible with `initializer?: Initializer<T>`.

Alternative: rename the property in `BuildResolverOptions` to `initializer` but type it as `Initializer<T> | ((initializer: Initializer<T>) => this)`?
That's hacky and would break actual option usage.

Alternative: Make `BuildResolver` not extend `BuildResolverOptions`, but instead extend a new interface that omits `initializer`. Let me create:

```ts
export interface BuildResolverOptions<T> extends ResolverOptions<T>, DisposableResolverOptions<T> {
  injectionMode?: InjectionModeType
  injector?: InjectorFunction
  initializer?: Initializer<T>
}

export interface BuildResolver<T> extends Resolver<T>, DisposableResolverOptions<T> {
  injectionMode?: InjectionModeType
  injector?: InjectorFunction
  setLifetime...
  ...
  initializer(initializer: Initializer<T>): this
}
```

But this loses `ResolverOptions<T>` fields like `name`, `lifetime`, `register`, `isLeakSafe` from BuildResolver type. But BuildResolver already has
`lifetime` via Resolver? `Resolver<T> extends ResolverOptions<T>`, so `Resolver<T>` includes ResolverOptions. Good. And `DisposableResolverOptions`
has `dispose`. So replacing `BuildResolverOptions<T>` with `Resolver<T> & DisposableResolverOptions<T>` in the extends preserves most fields except
`injectionMode` and `injector` which are declared directly. Actually `BuildResolverOptions` also has `injectionMode`, `injector`, and `initializer`.
If BuildResolver extends `Resolver<T>` (which extends ResolverOptions) and `DisposableResolverOptions<T>`, and declares `injectionMode`, `injector`,
`initializer` method, that's fine.

But then the `BuildResolverOptions` type still exists for opts params and includes `initializer?`. And `asFunction(fn, opts)` where opts is

parameter `instance: T` in target; source parameter `initializer: Initializer<T>`. Contravariance requires `Initializer<T>` assignable to `T`, which
is false. So conflict.

class DatabasePool {
  constructor() { this.connected = false }
  async connect() { this.connected = true }
  async disconnect() { this.connected = false }
}

async function main() {
  const container = createContainer()
  container.register({
    database: asClass(DatabasePool)
      .singleton()
      .initializer(async (instance) => {
        await instance.connect()
        return instance
      }),

  console.log('metrics', result.metrics.database)
  const db = container.resolve('database')
  console.log('connected', db.connected)
  const result2 = await container.initialize()

}

So the final resolver has `initializer` = builder method, not the function. This is why `runInitialization` calls the builder method (which creates a
resolver) rather than the actual initializer. Actually it calls `initializer(instance)` = `setInitializer(instance)`, which returns a new resolver;

function createBuildResolver<T, B extends Resolver<T>>(obj: B): BuildResolver<T> & B {
  ...

    setLifetime,
    inject,
    transient: ...,

```ts
function createDisposableResolver<T, B extends Resolver<T>>(obj: B): DisposableResolver<T> & B {
  function disposer(this: any, dispose: Disposer<T>) {
    return createDisposableResolver({ ...this, dispose })
  }
  return updateResolver(obj, { disposer })
}
```

Hmm, no initializer there. So how does `.initializer()` work in real awilix? Let me search my memory of the actual awilix resolvers.ts from v13:

I recall:
```ts
export function createBuildResolver<T, B extends Resolver<T>>(
  obj: B,
): BuildResolver<T> & B {
  ...
  function setInitializer(this: any, initializer: Initializer<T>) {
    return createBuildResolver({
      ...this,
      initializer,
    })
  }
  return updateResolver(obj, {
    setLifetime,
    inject,
    transient: partial(setLifetime, Lifetime.TRANSIENT),
    scoped: partial(setLifetime, Lifetime.SCOPED),
    singleton: partial(setLifetime, Lifetime.SINGLETON),
    setInjectionMode,
    proxy: partial(setInjectionMode, InjectionMode.PROXY),
    classic: partial(setInjectionMode, InjectionMode.CLASSIC),
    initializer: setInitializer,
  })
}
```

If that's the real code, then the same overwrite issue would exist. But maybe the actual execution reads `resolver.initializer` and it's the method —
that would be broken. So maybe real awilix does something else: they use a Symbol `INITIALIZER` to store the actual function, and `initializer` method
sets `[INITIALIZER]`, and `BuildResolverOptions` type uses a getter? Hmm.

Wait — actually I bet the real awilix stores the initializer function under the property name `initializer` but the fluid method is implemented
differently: when you call `.initializer(fn)`, it creates a new resolver with `initializer: fn`, but `createBuildResolver`'s `updateResolver` only
adds the method if... no.

Let me look at how the original `inject` works: data property `injector`, method `inject`. `dispose` data, method `disposer`. So clearly the
convention is to avoid name collision. So for initializer, perhaps the real awilix uses data property `initializer` and method `setInitializer`? But
README says `.initializer(fn)`. Let me check the actual README from the real awilix... The task description explicitly shows:

container.register({
  database: asClass(DatabasePool)

Solution: Store the actual initializer function under a **hidden symbol** or a differently-named property, and expose the fluid method `initializer`
that sets it. But then the type `BuildResolverOptions.initializer?: Initializer<T>` (if user passes opts) — we can store it under the same key too,
but the fluid method overwrites. To support both, we need a different strategy.

Let me design:
- Define a symbol `INITIALIZER = Symbol('initializer')` in resolvers.ts.
- The data property for the actual function is `[INITIALIZER]` (symbol key).
- The fluid method `initializer(initializer)` sets `this[INITIALIZER] = initializer` (via `{...this, [INITIALIZER]: initializer}`).
- `BuildResolverOptions` has `initializer?: Initializer<T>` as the *option* users can pass. When `makeOptions` creates the resolver, it has
`initializer` data property. But `createBuildResolver`'s `updateResolver` adds method `initializer` which would overwrite the data property. So
reading the actual function from `resolver.initializer` would still get the method, not the function.

So instead, the container should read `resolver[INITIALIZER]`. And `makeOptions` / `asFunction` / `asClass` should convert the `initializer` option
into `[INITIALIZER]`. Let's modify `asFunction` and `asClass` to normalize opts: if `opts.initializer` is provided, set `[INITIALIZER]` from it. Then
`createBuildResolver` can freely use the `initializer` method name without conflict, and execution reads the symbol.

But `BuildResolverOptions` would still expose `initializer?: Initializer<T>` for users passing options. The returned resolver would have
`[INITIALIZER]` data and `initializer` method. That works.

However, there's a subtlety: `updateResolver` spreads `...source`, which includes `initializer` method and `[INITIALIZER]` symbol. Symbol properties
are spread via object spread? Yes, object spread copies enumerable own symbol properties too. `[INITIALIZER]` is an enumerable own symbol property, so
it's copied. Good.

Also, when `setInitializer` does `{...this, [INITIALIZER]: initializer}`, the new object has the symbol set and the method `initializer` (from
`...this`) overwritten? Wait, `{...this, [INITIALIZER]: initializer}` — `...this` includes the old `initializer` method property and the old
`[INITIALIZER]` data. Then `[INITIALIZER]: initializer` sets new symbol value. Then `createBuildResolver` calls `updateResolver(obj, {... initializer:
setInitializer})` which sets the `initializer` method property. Good.

So I'll implement:
1. In resolvers.ts, export a symbol `INITIALIZER` (or keep internal; but container needs to read it — export it).
2. `asFunction` and `asClass`: after `opts = makeOptions(...)`, if `opts.initializer` exists, add `[INITIALIZER]: opts.initializer` to the result
object. Actually simpler: in `makeOptions` result, we have `initializer` property. We can set `const result = { resolve, ...opts, [INITIALIZER]:
opts.initializer }` for asFunction. For asClass similarly.
3. `setInitializer` method: `createBuildResolver({ ...this, [INITIALIZER]: initializer })`.
4. Container reads `(resolver as any)[INITIALIZER]` instead of `(resolver as any).initializer`.
5. Type: `BuildResolverOptions.initializer?: Initializer<T>` stays.
6. `BuildResolver` interface: currently has `initializer(initializer): this` method via `InitializerResolver` — fine.

But wait — there's still a runtime issue in `createBuildResolver`: when `updateResolver` spreads `source` (which includes `[INITIALIZER]` symbol), and
then sets `initializer` method, the symbol data is preserved. Good.

Also `createDisposableResolver` spreads `...this` preserving symbol. Good.

Let me also consider `asFunction(fn, { initializer: fn2 })` — `makeOptions` returns `{lifetime, ..., initializer: fn2}`. We then need to include
`[INITIALIZER]: fn2` in the result. But `createBuildResolver` would then add method `initializer` overwriting `initializer: fn2`. So reading
`[INITIALIZER]` is correct.

Let me implement. Also update container to use `INITIALIZER` symbol.

Let me also reconsider: maybe simpler to keep property name `initializer` for data and add method under a different approach where `updateResolver`
defines the method via a getter? No, symbol approach is clean.

cd /app && python3 - <<'PY'
p='src/resolvers.ts'
s=open(p).read()

# 1. Add INITIALIZER symbol after RESOLVER symbol
s=s.replace(
"""export const RESOLVER = Symbol('Awilix Resolver Config')
""",
"""export const RESOLVER = Symbol('Awilix Resolver Config')

/**
 * Symbol used to store the initializer function on a resolver, so the fluid
 * `initializer()` builder method can share the same public property name.
 */
export const INITIALIZER = Symbol('Awilix Resolver Initializer')
""")

# 2. asFunction: include INITIALIZER in result
s=s.replace(
"""  const resolve = generateResolve(fn)
  const result = {
    resolve,
    ...opts,
  }

  return createDisposableResolver(createBuildResolver(result))
}""",
"""  const resolve = generateResolve(fn)
  const result = {
    resolve,
    ...opts,
    [INITIALIZER]: (opts as any).initializer,
  }

  return createDisposableResolver(createBuildResolver(result))
}""")

# 3. asClass: include INITIALIZER in result
s=s.replace(
"""  const resolve = generateResolve(newClass, Type)
  return createDisposableResolver(
    createBuildResolver({
      ...opts,
      resolve,
    }),
  )
}""",
"""  const resolve = generateResolve(newClass, Type)
  return createDisposableResolver(
    createBuildResolver({
      ...opts,
      resolve,
      [INITIALIZER]: (opts as any).initializer,
    }),
  )
}""")

# 4. setInitializer method stores under INITIALIZER
s=s.replace(
"""  function setInitializer(this: any, initializer: Initializer<T>) {
    return createBuildResolver({
      ...this,
      initializer,
    })
  }""",
"""  function setInitializer(this: any, initializer: Initializer<T>) {
    return createBuildResolver({
      ...this,
      [INITIALIZER]: initializer,
    })
  }""")

open(p,'w').write(s)
print("resolvers patched")

REASONING_CONTENT
Basic functionality works. Now let me write comprehensive tests to validate all the requirements. Let me create a test file
`src/__tests__/container.initialize.test.ts` and run it. This will both serve as verification and as committed tests.

Let me enumerate the test cases based on the requirements:

1. **Basic API** — `asClass(...).singleton().initializer(...)`, `initialize({concurrency})` returns totalDuration, metrics.database.duration,
metrics.database.level.

2. **asFunction initializer** works.

3. **Dependency-aware levels**:
   - A depends on B (both with initializers) → B level 0, A level 1; B initializer completes before A starts.
   - Services with no dependencies at level 0.

4. **Concurrency within level**:
   - With `concurrency: 1`, two independent services initialize sequentially (max concurrent = 1).
   - With `concurrency` default, they can overlap.

5. **Initializer replacement**:
   - initializer returns a new object; resolve returns replacement; dispose uses replacement.

6. **Idempotent after success** — calling initialize twice returns same result / no re-run.

7. **Scoped containers independently**:
   - Parent singleton initializer called once; scope registers own service, scope.initialize() doesn't re-run parent initializer.

8. **Services without initializers resolvable before initialize**.

9. **Resolving uninitialized service throws AwilixNotInitializedError** with message containing "not initialized".

10. **Initialization failure**:
    - throws AwilixInitializationError with name + original message, err.cause is original error.
    - calls dispose on already-initialized services in reverse order.
    - same-level in-flight initializers complete before rollback begins.
    - disposer errors don't override original error.
    - re-initialization after failure rejects with /previously failed|Cannot re-initialize/.

11. **Circular dependency**:
    - throws AwilixResolutionError.
    - container not in failed state; initialize() can be retried after removing cycle (or after retry? Actually can't remove cycle without new
container; but test that a second initialize call after failure is NOT the "previously failed" error — i.e., it still throws AwilixResolutionError,
not Cannot re-initialize).

12. **Metrics level values**.

Let me also consider the "dispose in reverse order" test:
- Register A (level 0) and B (level 0) and C (level 1, depends on B).
- Let A initializer succeed, B initializer fail.
- C never runs (level 1 doesn't start).
- On rollback, only A is disposed (B failed, C not initialized).
- Assert dispose called on A.

For reverse order: register two independent services A and B at level 0, both succeed, then C at level 1 fails. Rollback should dispose B then A
(reverse of initialization order). But within a level with concurrency >1, completion order may be nondeterministic. To test deterministic reverse
order, use a chain: A (level 0) → B (level 1, depends on A) → C (level 2, depends on B) where C's initializer fails. Then initialized order: A, B.
Rollback should dispose B then A. This is deterministic.

For "in-flight same-level complete before rollback":
- Level 0 has A (slow, succeeds), B (fast, fails), and maybe track that when B fails, A is still running; after initialize rejects, both A and B have
completed (A's completion logged). Also C (level 1) must NOT start.
- Assert A completed before rejection. Use deferred promises.

Let me write the tests. I'll also need to import from `../awilix` or direct modules. Existing tests import from `../container`, `../resolvers`,
`../errors`. I'll do same.

Let me write the test file.

cd /app && cat > src/__tests__/container.initialize.test.ts <<'EOF'
import { createContainer, AwilixContainer } from '../container'
import {
  AwilixInitializationError,
  AwilixNotInitializedError,
  AwilixResolutionError,
} from '../errors'
import { asClass, asFunction } from '../resolvers'

class DatabasePool {
  connected = false
  async connect() {
    this.connected = true
  }
  async disconnect() {
    this.connected = false
  }
}

describe('container.initialize', () => {
  it('initializes a singleton asClass registration with an initializer', async () => {
    const container = createContainer()
    let initCalls = 0
    container.register({
      database: asClass(DatabasePool)
        .singleton()
        .initializer(async (instance) => {
          initCalls++
          await instance.connect()
          return instance
        }),
    })

    expect(() => container.resolve('database')).toThrow(
      AwilixNotInitializedError,
    )

    const result = await container.initialize({ concurrency: 5 })

    expect(typeof result.totalDuration).toBe('number')
    expect(typeof result.metrics.database.duration).toBe('number')
    expect(result.metrics.database.level).toBe(0)
    expect(initCalls).toBe(1)

    const db = container.resolve<DatabasePool>('database')
    expect(db.connected).toBe(true)

    // Idempotent after success.
    const again = await container.initialize()
    expect(again).toBe(result)
    expect(initCalls).toBe(1)
  })

  it('works with asFunction', async () => {
    const container = createContainer()
    const fn = asFunction((deps: any) => deps.value).initializer(
      async (instance) => ({ ...instance, initialized: true }),
    )
    container.register({ value: 42, svc: fn })

    const result = await container.initialize()
    expect(result.metrics.svc.level).toBe(0)
    const svc = container.resolve<{ value: number; initialized: boolean }>('svc')
    expect(svc.value).toBe(42)
    expect(svc.initialized).toBe(true)
  })

  it('allows the initializer to return a replacement', async () => {
    const container = createContainer()
    const replacement = { replaced: true }
    let disposedValue: any
    container.register({
      svc: asFunction(() => ({ original: true }))
        .singleton()
        .initializer(() => replacement)
        .disposer((value) => {
          disposedValue = value
        }),
    })

    await container.initialize()
    expect(container.resolve('svc')).toBe(replacement)

    await container.dispose()
    expect(disposedValue).toBe(replacement)
  })

  it('initializes in dependency order using levels', async () => {
    const container = createContainer()
    const order: string[] = []

    container.register({
      b: asFunction(() => ({}))
        .singleton()
        .initializer(async () => {
          await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 10))
          order.push('b')
        }),
      a: asFunction(({ b }: any) => ({ b }))
        .singleton()
        .initializer(async () => {
          order.push('a')
        }),
    })

    const result = await container.initialize()
    expect(order).toEqual(['b', 'a'])
    expect(result.metrics.b.level).toBe(0)
    expect(result.metrics.a.level).toBe(1)
  })

  it('limits concurrency within a level', async () => {
    const container = createContainer()
    let active = 0
    let maxActive = 0

    const mk = (name: string) =>
      asFunction(() => ({}))
        .singleton()
        .initializer(async () => {
          active++
          maxActive = Math.max(maxActive, active)
          await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 10))
          active--
        })

    container.register({
      a: mk('a'),
      b: mk('b'),
      c: mk('c'),
    })

    await container.initialize({ concurrency: 2 })
    expect(maxActive).toBeLessThanOrEqual(2)
    expect(maxActive).toBe(2)
  })

  it('allows services without initializers to be resolved before initialize', () => {
    const container = createContainer()
    container.register({ plain: asFunction(() => 42) })
    expect(container.resolve('plain')).toBe(42)
  })

  it('does not re-initialize parent singletons when a scoped container initializes', async () => {
    const container = createContainer()
    let parentInitCalls = 0
    container.register({
      parentSvc: asFunction(() => ({}))
        .singleton()
        .initializer(async () => {
          parentInitCalls++
        }),
    })

    await container.initialize()
    expect(parentInitCalls).toBe(1)

    const scope = container.createScope()
    let scopeInitCalls = 0
    scope.register({
      scopedSvc: asFunction(() => ({}))
        .scoped()
        .initializer(async () => {
          scopeInitCalls++
        }),
    })

    await scope.initialize()
    expect(parentInitCalls).toBe(1)
    expect(scopeInitCalls).toBe(1)
    expect(scope.resolve('parentSvc')).toBeDefined()
  })

  it('throws AwilixInitializationError with cause when an initializer fails', async () => {
    const container = createContainer()
    const boom = new Error('boom')
    container.register({
      svc: asFunction(() => ({})).singleton().initializer(async () => {
        throw boom
      }),
    })

    let error: unknown
    try {
      await container.initialize()
    } catch (err) {
      error = err
    }

    expect(error).toBeInstanceOf(AwilixInitializationError)
    const initError = error as AwilixInitializationError
    expect(initError.message).toContain('svc')
    expect(initError.message).toContain('boom')
    expect(initError.cause).toBe(boom)
  })

  it('disposes already-initialized services in reverse order on failure', async () => {
    const container = createContainer()
    const disposed: string[] = []
    const fail = new Error('fail')

    container.register({
      a: asFunction(() => ({})).singleton().initializer(async () => {}),
      b: asFunction(({ a }: any) => ({ a })).singleton().initializer(async () => {}),
      c: asFunction(({ b }: any) => ({ b })).singleton().initializer(async () => {
        throw fail
      }),
    })
    container.register({
      aDisposer: asValueHelper(disposed, 'a'),
      bDisposer: asValueHelper(disposed, 'b'),
    })

    const regA = container.getRegistration('a')
    const regB = container.getRegistration('b')
    ;(regA as any).dispose = () => {
      disposed.push('a')
    }
    ;(regB as any).dispose = () => {
      disposed.push('b')
    }

    await expect(container.initialize()).rejects.toThrow(AwilixInitializationError)
    expect(disposed).toEqual(['b', 'a'])
  })

  it('waits for in-flight initializers in a level before rolling back', async () => {
    const container = createContainer()
    const events: string[] = []
    let resolveSlow!: () => void
    const slowGate = new Promise<void>((r) => (resolveSlow = r))

    container.register({
      slow: asFunction(() => ({})).singleton().initializer(async () => {
        events.push('slow-start')
        await slowGate
        events.push('slow-end')
      }),
      fast: asFunction(() => ({})).singleton().initializer(async () => {
        events.push('fast-start')
        throw new Error('fast-fail')
      }),
      later: asFunction(({ slow }: any) => ({ slow })).singleton().initializer(async () => {
        events.push('later')
      }),
    })

    const promise = container.initialize()
    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 10))
    expect(events).toContain('slow-start')
    resolveSlow()
    await expect(promise).rejects.toThrow(AwilixInitializationError)
    expect(events).toContain('slow-end')
    expect(events).not.toContain('later')
  })

  it('ignores disposer errors during rollback', async () => {
    const container = createContainer()
    const boom = new Error('init-boom')
    container.register({
      good: asFunction(() => ({})).singleton().initializer(async () => {}),
      bad: asFunction(() => ({})).singleton().initializer(async () => {
        throw boom
      }),
    })
    ;(container.getRegistration('good') as any).dispose = () => {
      throw new Error('dispose-boom')
    }

    let error: unknown
    try {
      await container.initialize()
    } catch (err) {
      error = err
    }
    expect(error).toBeInstanceOf(AwilixInitializationError)
    expect((error as AwilixInitializationError).cause).toBe(boom)
  })

  it('cannot re-initialize after failure', async () => {
    const container = createContainer()
    container.register({
      svc: asFunction(() => ({})).singleton().initializer(async () => {
        throw new Error('nope')
      }),
    })

    await expect(container.initialize()).rejects.toThrow(AwilixInitializationError)
    await expect(container.initialize()).rejects.toThrow(
      /previously failed|Cannot re-initialize/,
    )
  })

  it('throws AwilixResolutionError for cycles without entering a failed state', async () => {
    const container = createContainer()
    container.register({
      a: asFunction(({ b }: any) => ({ b })).singleton().initializer(async () => {}),
      b: asFunction(({ a }: any) => ({ a })).singleton().initializer(async () => {}),
    })

    await expect(container.initialize()).rejects.toThrow(AwilixResolutionError)

    // Must not be a "previously failed" state - initialize can be retried.
    await expect(container.initialize()).rejects.toThrow(AwilixResolutionError)
    await expect(container.initialize()).rejects.not.toThrow(
      /previously failed|Cannot re-initialize/,
    )
  })
})

// Helper to avoid unused registration pattern; just adds a fake registration.
function asValueHelper(disposed: string[], name: string) {
  return asFunction(() => undefined).transient()
}

and we set `dispose` on it. That's fine but ugly. Better to use `.disposer(...)` method on the resolver. Let me rewrite the test file cleanly.

Let me rewrite the whole test file properly.
